16:I[44692,["/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/08cykig6vtx_p.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/07qi2okp-.yp9.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0frujd.eyhz4o.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0n4z.7yu76je-.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/09-o.77-p0p36.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/1304s0wcp2kce.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/0j1uy9t~zxcf0.js","/paper-daily-site/_next/static/chunks/14_5o-nbg8k_r.js"],"DailyView"]
17:T7f2,The core method is a precision-first, multi-stage filtering pipeline built on top of FineWeb-Edu, partitioning it into K–5 and Beyond-K–5 subsets. Stage one uses Age-of-Acquisition (AoA) as a lightweight proxy for developmental appropriateness. Since around 10% of FineWeb-Edu words lack AoA annotations, the authors impute missing values using a linear model over Zipf word frequency. They avoid overreacting to single hard words by discarding a sample only when more than 5% of its words exceed the target age threshold of 12. Stage two uses LLM-as-a-judge only on a subset, then trains scalable classifiers on those annotations. A cheap FastText model performs broad filtering, and a much stronger but roughly 50x more expensive ModernBERT model is applied to about 266M samples whose grade-band prediction agrees with the AoA assignment. This is a practical cascade: full-corpus LLM labeling would cost about $46M with Gemini Flash pricing, so LLMJ is used to create supervision, not to label everything directly. Stage three adds rule-based symbolic filtering for formulas and notation that are rare, hard for classifiers to learn reliably, and often clear indicators of beyond-elementary content, such as quadratic expressions and symbols like ∑, ∫, and ∂. This step removes only about 0.1% of already retained documents, so it mainly cleans a residual tail rather than acting as a broad math filter. Stage four tightens the boundary further using term-based exclusion. Because the post-filter corpus is still larger than the target training size, the authors do not subsample uniformly. Instead, they identify terms disproportionately associated with Beyond-K–5 material and remove candidate documents containing them. The logic is explicit: sparse occurrences can still teach factual knowledge, so controlled-exposure studies should prefer over-filtering to leakage. Validation is done on CommonCoreText, a held-out dataset with ground-truth grade labels. The overall design choice is clear: precision over recall.18:T46a,The most important result is not downstream performance but boundary validation. On CommonCoreText, the pipeline reduces Beyond-K–5 retention to 0% while preserving about 35% of K–5 content. That 35% retention is intentionally low: the authors accept heavy false negatives in exchange for high confidence that the retained corpus respects the developmental boundary. The final corpus, LittleCurriculum, contains 88B tokens, and the authors train a 5B-parameter model, LittleLearner, from scratch on it. The model is reported to have enough general language competence for open-ended evaluation while still exhibiting clear knowledge and capability boundaries. The paper’s experimental emphasis is therefore not “how strong is the model,” but “how cleanly can we control exposure and observe the resulting gaps.” Another useful practical lesson is the cost structure of the pipeline: LLMJ for sparse supervision, FastText for cheap broad filtering, ModernBERT for high-quality reclassification, and symbolic rules for residual failure modes. That cascade is directly relevant to large-scale pretraining data curation.19:T416,The work reframes curriculum depth growing as a block-wise inherited initialization process, where normalization serves not only to stabilize gradient propagation across the full stack, but also to shape the forward distribution passed from trained prefix blocks to newly appended blocks. Pre-norm applies normalization only before each sub-layer and does not explicitly normalize residual stream outputs across block boundaries, while post-norm applies normalization after every residual update, delivering more stable representation scale at block boundaries. The study uses a frozen Qwen3-8B as the teacher, with a 9-layer expandable block-stacked student for controlled distillation, keeping parameter counts identical across normalization variants. It compares validation cross-entropy for pre-norm and post-norm under both joint training and curriculum depth growth, and isolates the mechanism via block-removal analysis, block-boundary activation scale diagnostics, and a matched-token post-joint control to rule out confounding variables.1a:T44d,Sniper is a two-stage structured pruning framework (depth then width).
- Mixed-granularity decomposition: each layer is represented by attention block A_l and MLP block M_l, plus a composite item T_l meaning “keep the whole layer”. A mutual-exclusion constraint prevents selecting T_l together with its sub-blocks.
- Depth pruning as 0/1 knapsack: each component x has weight w(x) (parameter count) and value v(x) (importance). Given a parameter budget C, maximize total value under the capacity constraint. The solver uses dynamic programming; to make it tractable for large C, parameter counts are discretized by a factor α, reducing complexity to O(N·⌊C/α⌋).
- Importance estimation: an iterative marginal-contribution scheme where v(x) is derived from the divergence in output logits when removing x on a calibration batch.
- Width pruning stage: applied after the depth-selected backbone to fine-tune the final model to match the target compression ratio closely.
- Metric: CRAFT (Compression Ratio Adherence Factor) quantifies how well a method meets the requested compression budget.1b:T542,The method has three parts. First, the authors build a black-box calibrated cost model for the deployed expert kernel. Per-expert latency is modeled with a two-regime structure: a fixed activation floor for instantiating an expert replica, plus a token-dependent term that becomes relevant beyond an inflection point. They also explicitly model grouped-GEMM M-tile padding, which explains why splitting one expert across multiple replicas can create extra work instead of merely redistributing it. Second, they formulate per-batch dispatch as a fixed-charge makespan minimization problem over routed tokens and replica placements. The paper proves the problem is already NP-hard in a small setting, while showing that prior token-balancing and activation-balancing methods emerge as degenerate limits when certain terms vanish. This is a clean unification of earlier heuristics. Third, they propose tempo_fast, a practical near-optimal solver. It uses cost-aware seeding, augmenting-chain activation rebalancing for the flat regime, bottleneck-focused local search with partial migrations for the linear regime, and an ensemble that scores candidate solutions such as token-LP and round-robin under the same calibrated model. The strength of the algorithm is that each move is tied to a measured hardware effect rather than a surrogate objective.1c:T449,The most informative results are the measurement studies. On two generations of datacenter GPUs, expert FFN latency stays nearly flat up to about 156–168 tokens per expert, then transitions to a more linear region. The fitted activation floor b ranges from 1.74 microseconds to 14.8 microseconds depending on expert shape, an 8.5x spread, with mean fit error around 4–8%. In real decode traces, 92–100% of batches contain both regimes simultaneously: a few hot experts carry roughly 91% of tokens, while many activated experts still sit in the flat regime. Holding the batch and placement fixed and varying only the split, deployed proxy policies differ by 1.2–1.6x in modeled MoE block time, with p95 reaching 1.7x; each proxy pays a 30–70% penalty away from its preferred regime. On the optimization side, tempo_fast gets within about 1.02% of optimal or near-optimal references, which is strong given the combinatorial objective. The phase-diagram analysis is also useful: the winning policy flips along predictable boundaries, so no fixed proxy is robust across operating conditions.1d:T48b,Dual-Flow Transformer uses an asymmetric shared-KV design. The primary flow is a full standard causal Transformer that processes the entire prompt independently and produces the only persistent KV cache, with no dependency on the auxiliary flow. The auxiliary flow only reads the primary flow's states and KV cache, never writes persistent state, and never feeds back into the primary flow. The two flows share all attention, MLP, and output matrices, with only separate token embeddings and lightweight learned coupling vectors. The auxiliary flow is not evaluated across the full prompt during prefill — it only runs at the final prompt token position and each subsequent generated token, so prefill compute cost and KV cache size are nearly identical to a standard Transformer. Training uses a mixture likelihood over the two flows' next-token distributions as the supervision signal. For MoE variants, the auxiliary flow reuses the primary flow's selected experts with its own mixture weights, and the expert fan-outs of the two flows can be configured independently to create a three-way trade-off between prefill cost, decode cost, and prediction quality.1e:T428,Three commitments: (1) agents edit a typed Cake IR rather than raw CUDA; the IR records warp roles, buffer staging depth, barrier/handoff structure, and instruction operands, while mechanical details—barrier addresses, phase bits, TMEM offsets, descriptor encodings, warp identity—are derived by lowering rather than authored; (2) the compiler returns localized correctness and performance diagnostics instead of a pass/fail bit, so cheap static analysis filters candidates before GPU time is spent; (3) the harness itself is an evolution target: repeated failures become verifier rules, calibration tasks, or new primitives, gated by corpus tests. Cake IR was not predefined but grown bottom-up through agent-driven abstraction discovery over a corpus of production kernels. Two entry points are supported: continuing evolution of an existing production kernel (FlashInfer/CUTLASS), or starting from a high-level description/Triton implementation and letting agents choose warp specialization, layout, and pipeline structure. Targets Ampere through Blackwell.1f:T4b8,The Wiggle Framework applies a graduated stress test on fixed items, judges, and grading schemes. A temperature-0, no-pressure verdict serves as the L0 anchor for each item. A wiggle is defined as a verdict flip on binary scales, and on Likert 1–5 scales as a move of two or more points (crossing the midpoint, or jumping from the midpoint to an extreme), excluding minor numerical drift. Stability is decomposed into three dimensions. Mechanical Consistency tests verdict retention under information-free perturbations: 10 identical decoding runs, random-seed strings appended to the system prompt, and swapped argument order. Single-turn Conviction tests whether the verdict survives one substantive challenge immediately after the decision. Multi-turn Persistence tests stability under sustained, adaptive pushback over multiple turns. Each wiggle is further labeled as corrective (toward ground truth) or corrupting (away from ground truth), separating stability from accuracy direction. The study covers 9 frontier models on 14 judging tasks spanning safety classification, toxicity detection, red-teaming, AI-writing detection, and political-response evaluation, under both binary and Likert grading.20:T5c0,The paper introduces Constraint Saturation Evaluation (CSE). Two design choices define it. First, every constraint must be deterministically verifiable, with no LLM judge in the loop. This keeps the evaluator stable exactly where composition becomes hard. Second, the benchmark varies the number of simultaneous constraints k systematically from 1 to 12, instead of testing only a few fixed compositions. CSE contains 36 constraint types across 8 processing dimensions, ranging from local lexical constraints to document-level structure, relational reasoning, and self-referential tasks. Each verifier returns both a binary pass/fail decision and a continuous compliance score in [0,1]. The benchmark construction also includes a composability vetting stage, which is important: a constraint that implicitly locks multiple output dimensions would distort high-k behavior and confound the analysis. The paper then analyzes three levels of behavior: per-constraint pass rate as k increases, probe-level joint success, and co-failure structure. The main empirical finding is that failures are only weakly correlated, with mean phi around +0.067, so joint success is well approximated by multiplying per-constraint success rates. The authors also show that not all constraints degrade equally: constraints that require sustained maintenance during generation deteriorate much faster than local lexical constraints, while near-binary decision constraints are relatively robust.21:T443,The evaluation covers 15 models from 8 families, 36 constraint types, k from 1 to 12, and 369,753 total constraint checks. Four results stand out. First, per-constraint pass rate follows a simple multiplicative law, 72.0% × 0.922^(k−1), with held-out MAE of only 0.2 percentage points. So the degradation is highly regular rather than noisy. Second, even mild per-constraint decay leads to severe collapse in joint success: by k=9, probe-level success falls below 2%; a model with about 41% per-constraint success at k=8 achieves only 5.7% full success on all eight constraints. Third, even the strongest model drops below 50% probe-level success at only 7 constraints, and 12 of the 15 models cross that threshold at 3 constraints or fewer. Fourth, structural constraints lose baseline capability about 2.0x faster per added constraint than lexical ones, with a 95% CI of [1.9, 2.3], and the comprehension-maintenance gap correlates with degradation rate at rho = -0.584. In practical terms, the more a constraint must be actively maintained throughout generation, the earlier it breaks.22:T42f,Reasoning Jury is a two-phase evaluation system for step-segmented reasoning traces marked with [STEP-x] tokens. Phase 1 is independent judgment: a panel of jurors from diverse model families each read the problem and reasoning trace independently, and output a structured defect list including description, affected step IDs, severity, and supporting evidence. This ensures no anchoring effect and decorrelated judgment biases across jurors. Phase 2 is consensus building, with two available modes: Consolidation mode passes the original trace and all Phase 1 verdicts to a judge model to produce a unified consensus. Deliberation mode uses a moderator to run multi-turn debates between jurors, who discuss defect points until consensus is reached or discussion stalls. The moderator never accesses the original problem or reasoning trace — it only maintains consensus state at the claim level, selects speakers, and decides termination timing, then synthesizes the final consensus. All outputs are grounded to specific step markers for auditability and actionability.23:T424,For reconstruction, the V-JEPA 2.1 variant achieves 2.13 rFVD on K600, outperforming all evaluated large-scale pretrained video VAEs; the DINOv3-L variant reaches 6.12 rFVD on UCF101, slightly below Wan2.1 VAE at 6.05. For semantic preservation, the DINOv3-L variant reaches 89.13% top-1 classification accuracy on UCF101, compared to 30.83% for the strongest VAE baseline. For generation, the best-performing variant achieves gFVD scores of 117.86 on UCF101 and 19.16 on K600, outperforming all VAE baselines and converging up to 6× faster under matched training settings. Additional analysis shows that rFVD (reconstruction FVD) has very low correlation with gFVD (generation FVD), with Pearson correlation coefficients of only 0.200 on UCF101 and 0.473 on K600. In contrast, the proposed tFVD (temporal coherence FVD, evaluated by decoding interpolated trajectories in latent space) has significantly higher correlation with generation quality, reaching 0.919 on K600 and 0.621 on UCF101, making it a more reliable evaluation metric for video autoencoders.24:T7f9,The main idea is to replace generic richness proxies with per-sample checklists. A rubric is not a reference answer. It is a structured list of required and optional key points for a given question, plus a one-sentence completeness summary. The model’s richness is scored by rubric coverage, while grounding is scored with a sentence-level support signal. The paper uses “grounding” broadly to cover both faithfulness to provided context and factual support from reliable world knowledge.

The study compares five reward variants built from three ingredients: grounding, rubric coverage, and pairwise relevance. The important result is that the composition matters as much as the ingredients. Pure grounding rewards improve support but push the model toward shorter, safer answers. Pure rubric rewards preserve coverage but weaken support. Multiplicative gating inherits the conservatism of grounding-heavy objectives. Simple additive combinations help but only partially. The best balance in their experiments comes from a soft three-term reward: a non-gating grounding term plus rubric coverage plus pairwise relevance.

Rubric construction is also part of the contribution. Instead of relying on a single rubric writer, the authors use a multi-generator union pipeline: GPT-5.4 and Gemini-3-Flash-Preview independently draft candidate rubrics, and GPT-OSS-120B merges and deduplicates them into a final union rubric. The goal is to reduce omissions from any single generator while keeping the rubric compact enough for practical judging. The RL training pool contains 4k prompts, split evenly between grounded prompts with context and open prompts from WildChat, so the model does not learn a purely reference-grounded, refusal-heavy policy.

Methodologically, this is less about a new RL algorithm and more about reward specification. The paper cleanly separates two objectives that are often conflated in long-form generation: whether each sentence is supported, and whether the answer covers the information the question demands.25:T60b,The experiments use two open-weight model families: Qwen3-4B and DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B. The comparison is controlled carefully: same base checkpoint, same prompt pool, same rollout setup, same evaluation splits, same judges; only the reward changes.

Training uses 4,000 RL prompts: 2,000 grounded prompts with context from a reliability-oriented pool, and 2,000 open prompts from WildChat. Evaluation covers FACTS Grounding, LongFact, FActScore closed-book and open-book, CL-bench-style checklist tasks, and out-of-distribution settings such as creative writing and pairwise preference.

The main findings are consistent. First, strict grounding-only rewards quickly shorten responses within a small number of RL steps, showing that the model discovers refusal or under-answering as the safest way to avoid unsupported claims. Second, rubric-only rewards preserve coverage much better, especially on checklist completion and open-ended writing, but grounding degrades. Third, hard fact-rubric gating remains weak out of distribution because it preserves the same conservative bias. Fourth, the soft three-component reward gives the best trade-off between in-distribution grounding and out-of-distribution completion / preference behavior.

The excerpt provides the key setup numbers but not the full score tables, so it would be unsafe to invent exact gains. The important experimental lesson is structural: the shape of the reward determines the shape of the learned policy, and all-or-nothing grounding terms naturally favor saying less.26:T4a0,Start with the introduction, especially the refusal-to-richness trade-off. Then read the reward design section carefully, because the paper lives or dies on how grounding, rubric coverage, and pairwise relevance are defined and combined. After that, go straight to the main result tables. The key is not a single best score, but the directional pattern across in-distribution grounding and out-of-distribution checklist / creative-writing behavior.

A 25-minute skim should focus on four things: 1) the formal definition of rubric coverage, 2) the table or formulas for the five reward variants, 3) the training curve showing response length collapse under FACT-ONLY RL, and 4) the OOD evaluation results. The most interesting follow-up questions are whether the reward overfits to rubric-writer preferences, how stable the multi-generator union pipeline is, and whether the same reward design transfers to reasoning RL, RAG answer generation, or continual post-training. For readers interested in the pretrain interface, the practical lesson is simple: if the training signal only punishes errors and does not reward completeness, the model will shrink its answer support by default.27:T8bc,方法核心是 CROP：Counterfactual Relevance for On-Policy Distillation。流程分三层。第一层，离线为每个原始 prompt 构造一个 matched triplet：原始输入 x、语义等价的 paraphrase x_para、以及只改动一个关键任务条件的 counterfactual x_cf。这里的设计意图很明确：paraphrase 用来估计模型对表述改写的敏感度，counterfactual 用来估计模型对任务语义变化的敏感度。第二层，在学生当前策略生成的同一条 rollout y 上做重打分，而不是让不同 prompt 各自重新采样响应。也就是固定原始 rollout 的前缀，分别计算在 x、x_para、x_cf 条件下，各 token 位置的下一词分布差异。这样做的好处是把比较集中在“同一 realized trajectory 上的条件分布变化”，减少由不同采样路径带来的噪声。第三层，用 counterfactual sensitivity 减去 paraphrase sensitivity，得到 token 级 relevance score，再在 batch 级固定预算下做 hard selection，只对得分最高的一部分 sampled tokens 施加教师蒸馏损失，teacher target 和 OPD 目标本身不变。

分布差异的具体实现上，论文使用 top-K JSD，并带 residual bucket。这样做是为了避免全词表 JSD 的高成本，同时又不至于因为只看 top-K 而丢掉剩余概率质量。这个设计比直接比较 argmax 或 top-1 logit 更稳，因为 relevance 关心的是整个局部分布如何随语义变化而变化，而不是单一 token 是否翻转。

和最接近的 CREDIT 相比，区别主要有三点。其一，CREDIT 用 unrelated batch queries 做对比，语义变化和其他 nuisance factors 混在一起；CROP 用 paraphrase/counterfactual 配对，把“语义不变的表面改写”和“关键条件改变”分开。其二，CROP 在固定 rollout 上比较条件分布，强调 controlled contrast；其三，最终分数是 counterfactual sensitivity 减 paraphrase sensitivity，显式做了语义校准，而不是把任何输入敏感都当成高价值监督。论文还强调，CROP 不是在估计严格因果意义上的 token causal effect，而是在预算受限 OPD 里构造一个可操作、模型内部、对比受控的 relevance proxy。28:T7e8,CROP, short for Counterfactual Relevance for On-Policy Distillation, builds a token relevance score from controlled prompt contrasts. For each original prompt x, the method constructs an offline matched triplet: the original prompt x, a meaning-preserving paraphrase x_para, and a counterfactual prompt x_cf that changes one task-relevant condition. The student first generates a rollout y from its current policy on the original prompt. CROP then keeps that realized rollout fixed and rescoring is performed under all three prompts on the same response prefixes. This is an important design choice: the comparison is over conditional next-token distributions along the same trajectory, rather than over independently sampled responses, which reduces variance from trajectory mismatch.

At each token position, CROP measures how much the model’s next-token distribution changes under the counterfactual prompt and subtracts how much it changes under the paraphrase prompt. Intuitively, sensitivity to the counterfactual captures dependence on task semantics, while sensitivity to the paraphrase captures dependence on superficial wording. The resulting score is a calibrated relevance proxy rather than a generic input-sensitivity measure. Tokens are then selected globally under a fixed batch-level budget, and only those selected sampled tokens receive the teacher distillation loss. The teacher target and the OPD objective remain unchanged; only the allocation of supervision changes.

Distribution shift is computed with a top-K JSD plus a residual bucket. This is a practical compromise: full-vocabulary divergence is expensive, but top-K alone can ignore tail mass. Compared with CREDIT, the closest prior work discussed by the authors, CROP uses controlled original/paraphrase/counterfactual triplets instead of unrelated batch queries, and explicitly subtracts paraphrase sensitivity from counterfactual sensitivity. That makes the signal more targeted to semantic dependence rather than generic prompt variation.29:T5e7,The empirical setup includes two teacher-student settings evaluated within the same budgeted OPD framework. The headline result reported in the paper is that CROP improves aggregate performance by 1.92 and 2.96 points over the strongest non-CROP selector. For a token-selection method inside distillation, that is a meaningful gain.

The most convincing evidence comes from matched token-selection controls. Under the same supervision budget, selecting the highest-relevance tokens consistently outperforms random selection, while selecting the lowest-relevance tokens performs substantially worse. This matters because it validates the ranking induced by the relevance score itself, not just the end-to-end training recipe.

Ablations show that counterfactual sensitivity is the primary signal and paraphrase calibration adds further benefit. In other words, semantic sensitivity already identifies useful supervision locations, and subtracting paraphrase sensitivity removes positions that react to wording changes rather than task content. The paper also adapts CREDIT into the same selector framework for a cleaner comparison.

The main limitations are also visible from the setup. The method depends on constructing and validating prompt triplets offline, which introduces extra pipeline cost. It also assumes that a meaningful task-relevant counterfactual can be defined for the prompt. That assumption is easier to satisfy in structured QA-style tasks than in open-ended generation or long-form dialogue.2a:T41a,The key observation is that a module of d interchangeable units (hidden units, attention heads, MoE experts, conv channels) is invariant under relabeling, i.e. has S_d symmetry. Under small initialization scale ε and a vanishing-gradient-at-origin condition, a Taylor expansion about the origin has its cubic term killed by symmetry, leaving the leading nontrivial term Tr[WW^T A(x)] plus a linear μ^T g(x) term that vanishes for most practical architectures. All architecture specificity is compressed into a p×p symmetric structure matrix A(x) whose sparsity pattern and couplings are fixed by the architecture and input; the order parameter is M=WW^T rather than individual weights. Notably, for attention the value and readout blocks appear at quadratic order, while Q and K enter only at quartic order—a concrete prediction about early-training dynamics. The same quadratic form is then used to derive a power law for excess loss versus rescaled training time, with the same spectral structure argued to govern model- and data-size scaling.2b:T494,The behavioral evaluation uses a core100 subset from HOB, stratified over a 4-by-5 taxonomy of heuristics and constraints. Fourteen models are evaluated, spanning Claude, GPT, Gemini, Grok, DeepSeek-R1, Kimi, Llama, Qwen, and GPT-OSS families. The quartet metric separates over-activation and under-activation. Several models give constraint-heavy answers even when the constraint is removed, with CBI around +0.13 to +0.29. GPT-OSS-20B and GPT-OSS-120B show under-activation, with CBI around -0.10 to -0.19. Mechanistic tests on open-weight models show a split: in Qwen3-14B, patching constraint information from an Explicit prompt into a failing Active prompt shifts the gold-shortcut gap by +6.4 nats without causing the removed controls to flip; in GPT-OSS-20B, a layer-20 probe decodes the constraint at about 88% accuracy, but patching changes the answer by only about -0.07 nats. Prompted mitigations, including chain-of-thought, precondition listing, goal decomposition, and counterfactual checking, mostly increase removed-pair harm. Across 40 model-strategy cells, mean active gain is only +0.01 to +0.04 CBI, while mean removed harm is about +0.44 to +0.47 CBI.2c:T776,The setup is simple but careful. The authors freeze model weights and intervene only at inference time. They study Qwen 3.5-2B (24 layers, d=2048) and Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct (16 layers, d=2048).

The language axis is built layer by layer. First, they collect hidden states from 20k held-out bilingual sentence pairs and subtract the layerwise mean. Second, they run PCA on the centered activations and keep the top 150 components. Third, instead of taking PC1, they score each principal component by Cohen’s d between the two language distributions and select the component with the largest absolute separation, orienting it so the positive direction corresponds to the target language. This gives one unit vector per layer. Compared with a raw mean-difference vector or a probe weight, this procedure constrains the search to high-variance subspaces and then explicitly selects the component that best separates the language pair.

They then perform two intervention types. Steering adds a scaled offset along the language axis during the forward pass. Ablation removes the projection onto that axis. Steering tests sufficiency: can the model be pushed into another language? Ablation tests necessity: does removing the axis impair the original language behavior? The paper also includes strong controls, especially equal-magnitude random perturbations, to rule out the trivial explanation that any large perturbation can flip the output.

Two technical findings stand out. First, the geometry is pair-specific rather than a single universal “language direction”: English-Chinese and English-Spanish require distinct axes. Second, the effect is strongly layer-dependent. Mid layers often resist steering, while later layers are more sensitive, suggesting that language realization is not decided only at the final logits, but surface-form commitment becomes much easier to alter in later representations.2d:T58f,The experimental sweep is fairly large for an activation intervention paper. The main evaluation uses FLORES-200, with 500 English-Chinese pairs and 100 English-Spanish pairs. Axis calibration uses 20k held-out sentence pairs from MultiUN. In total, the study reports 1.26 million generations.

The main results are qualitative but consistent. Steering along the PCA+Cohen’s d language axis reliably induces language switching in both cross-script and same-script settings, while random perturbations of the same magnitude generally do not. That supports specificity rather than generic disruption. Ablation weakens or disrupts language-consistent generation, which supports the claim that the axis is not only decodable but causally used. Layer sweeps reveal a non-uniform control profile: some mid layers are relatively resistant even under stronger steering, whereas later layers are much easier to redirect. The paper also characterizes a boundary between coherent steering and generation collapse, showing that useful control exists only within a finite intervention range.

From the full text provided here, the paper emphasizes robustness of the phenomenon and the control design more than a single headline metric. The available hard numbers are model sizes, layer counts, calibration size, evaluation size, and total generations; the excerpt does not include a full table of exact switch rates or quality scores.2e:T421,For a quick read, start with the distinction from probing papers: the key question is not whether language is linearly recoverable, but whether changing that direction changes behavior. Then read the method section on how the language axis is constructed. After that, go straight to the layerwise steering results, which are likely the most informative figures. Finally, check the random-direction controls and the ablation results, because those are what make the causal claim credible.

A 20–30 minute skim should cover: 1) axis construction, 2) main steering results, 3) random perturbation controls, and 4) comparisons across language pairs and layers. The most interesting follow-up questions are why pair-specific axes work better than a shared language coordinate, and whether the mid-layer resistance / late-layer sensitivity pattern reflects a semantic-to-surface division of labor. For pretraining-oriented readers, the natural next question is whether this geometry depends on tokenizer design, multilingual data mixture, or instruction tuning.2f:T5a7,The study uses paired comparisons between base and instruction-tuned models from three families: Qwen2.5-7B, Llama-3.1-8B, and Mistral-7B-v0.3. Evaluation is done on three multiple-choice QA benchmarks: ARC-Easy, MMLU, and CommonsenseQA. Confidence is measured in two complementary ways. First, the authors compute likelihood-based answer uncertainty using the entropy of normalized answer probabilities over the candidate options. Second, they elicit verbalized confidence with a two-stage protocol: the model first selects an answer via likelihood, then in a separate pass reports a numerical probability that the chosen answer is correct.

For rationale diversity, the model generates K=5 chain-of-thought rationales per question. Diversity is measured using Unique-2, which captures lexical richness through distinct bigram proportion, and Self-BLEU, reported as 1-SelfBLEU so that larger values indicate greater variation across sampled rationales. A strong part of the design is the controlled comparison restricted to examples where base and instruction-tuned models choose the same answer, with rationale length matched across variants. This removes two major confounders: answer switching and generation-length effects.

The paper is careful not to collapse confidence, calibration, and rationale diversity into a single notion. Instead, it treats them as separate observables and asks whether they move together after instruction tuning.30:T5fe,The main empirical finding is consistent across all model families and benchmarks: instruction tuning increases confidence, but the increase is not matched by equally consistent gains in accuracy or calibration. Choice entropy drops substantially in every setting, for example from 0.430 to 0.131 for Qwen on MMLU and from 0.736 to 0.268 for Mistral on CSQA. Verbalized confidence also rises sharply, including from 49.2% to 90.4% for Llama on ARC-Easy and from 46.8% to 91.1% on CSQA. Yet accuracy does not move in parallel; Llama on ARC-Easy stays at 82.2% accuracy despite the large confidence increase.

Rationale diversity behaves less uniformly. Unique-2 changes are mixed across models and datasets, with some increases and some decreases. For instance, Mistral on CSQA increases from 0.719 to 0.750, while other settings show declines. By contrast, 1-SelfBLEU decreases across all models and benchmarks, meaning repeated rationale generations become more similar to one another after instruction tuning. The largest drop is reported for Mistral on ARC-Easy, from 0.813 to 0.626.

The controlled same-answer, length-matched analysis is especially informative. It shows that lower answer uncertainty or higher verbalized confidence does not consistently align with either lower or higher lexical diversity. So the story is not simply that instruction tuning makes models more confident because it makes their rationales uniformly more templated. It is difficult to select downstream tasks or validation sets representative of a model's general capabilities, and reliance on task performance at intermediate checkpoints complicates comparisons across training. We propose a measure of training data influence that does not require selecting a downstream task or validation set as the attribution target. Specifically, we define an example's influence","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T17:36:49Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13515.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文想解决的是预训练过程中“单条数据到底在什么时候有用”的度量问题。传统 influence 方法通常要指定下游任务、验证集或某个目标样本，这在 LLM 预训练里不稳定：早期 checkpoint 还没有形成完整能力，后期能力又混在一起，很难用同一个 benchmark 解释全程的数据作用。","importance":"对预训练数据研究的启发在于，它给出了一个不依赖下游任务的训练数据影响度量。影响被定义为一次更新把参数推向最终预训练参数的程度，因此可以在同一条训练轨迹上比较不同阶段、不同数据类型的贡献。这个信号适合用来分析 curriculum、data mixture annealing、阶段性数据重采样，但它不是因果数据估值，也不等价于“提升最终能力”。它依赖最终 checkpoint 作为参照点，所以更像是在解释一条既定训练轨迹，而不是直接回答“如果删除这批数据会怎样”。","technical_approach":"方法的核心很简单：把最终参数 θ* 当作公共参考点，计算第 t 步更新前后到 θ* 的平方 L2 距离变化。mini-batch 贡献为 S_t - S_{t+1}，展开后是 2 Δ_t^T(θ* - θ_t) - ||Δ_t||²。第一项衡量更新方向是否朝向最终模型，第二项惩罚更新步长本身。这样，一个 batch 可能是负贡献，表示它在参数空间里暂时把模型推离最终点。论文进一步在 SGD 假设下做 example-level 分解，用单样本梯度近似每个样本的影响。作者也明确指出，真实 LLM 预训练常用 Adam，单样本对更新的精确贡献会受优化器状态影响，因此这里的 per-example influence 是可计算的近似，而 mini-batch 级别的距离变化定义不依赖优化器分解。","experiments":"实验覆盖 Pythia 和 PolyPythia 系列的 18 个配置，每条轨迹包含 154 个 checkpoint。主要观察是数据影响随训练阶段系统变化：文学类数据在早期更能把模型推向最终参数，而 STEM 数据在后期影响增强。论文还检查了 checkpoint 近似、不同 reference endpoint、不同模型配置和不同 attribution target 下结果是否稳定。关键结论不是某个数据域始终更好，而是数据域的有效性有明显时间结构，这支持阶段感知的数据配比和课程设计。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 20–30 分钟。重点看 Section 3 的定义和推导，尤其是 influence 展开式中 alignment term 与 norm penalty 的含义；再看 Section 4 的时间演化图，理解不同数据域在训练阶段上的变化；最后看 Section 5 的可靠性实验，判断这个指标在 checkpoint 稀疏、endpoint 改变和模型配置变化时是否还能保持结论。需要特别留意两个边界：参数空间 L2 距离是否是合适几何，以及 Adam 下 example-level 分解的近似误差。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper studies how to measure the influence of individual pretraining examples without choosing a downstream task, validation set, or target prediction. Task-specific influence is hard to compare across pretraining because early checkpoints may not yet express the relevant capability, while later checkpoints mix many capabilities together.","importance":"The useful contribution is a task-agnostic signal for analyzing data contribution along a fixed pretraining run. An example is influential if its update moves the model closer to the final pretrained parameters. This is relevant for data curriculum, mixture scheduling, and stage-aware data selection. The signal should not be read as causal data valuation or as a direct measure of downstream utility. It explains a realized trajectory relative to its endpoint.","technical_approach":"The method uses the final parameters θ* as a common reference. For step t, it measures the reduction in squared parameter distance, S_t - S_{t+1}, where S_t = ||θ* - θ_t||². Expanding the update gives 2 Δ_t^T(θ* - θ_t) - ||Δ_t||². The first term measures alignment with the direction toward the final model, and the second term penalizes update magnitude. A batch can have negative contribution if it moves away from the endpoint. For example-level attribution, the paper uses an SGD-style decomposition with per-example gradients. This is a tractable approximation; under Adam, the exact contribution also depends on optimizer states.","experiments":"The analysis is applied to 18 configurations from the Pythia and PolyPythia suites, each with 154 checkpoints. The main empirical pattern is stage dependence: literature data are more influential earlier in training, while STEM data become more influential later. The paper also studies robustness to checkpoint approximation, choice of reference endpoint, model configuration, and attribution target. The main result is not a static ranking of domains, but the observation that data influence changes systematically over the training trajectory.","reading_guide":"A 20–30 minute skim is enough. Read Section 3 for the distance-reduction definition and the alignment-versus-norm decomposition. Then inspect the temporal influence plots in Section 4. Section 5 is important for judging whether the metric is stable under endpoint and checkpoint choices. The main caveats to track are the use of Euclidean parameter geometry and the SGD approximation for per-example influence in Adam-trained models.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"任务无关的数据影响力测量方法实用性强，训练阶段领域影响力偏移的发现可直接指导数据课程设计，值得复现用于内部数据评估。","en":"Task-agnostic training data influence measurement is highly practical; the domain shift in influence across training stages directly informs data curriculum design, worth reproducing for internal data evaluation."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Nara Institute of Science and Technology","The University of Tokyo","Affiliation:Chaoran Liu","Affiliation:Max Müller-Eberstein","Affiliation:[0.5em]Nara Institute of Science and Technology","Affiliation:The University of Tokyo"],"authors":["Yuto Nishida","Hirokazu Kiyomaru","Yusuke Oda","Takashi Kodama","Chaoran Liu","Daisuke Kawahara","Yusuke Miyao","Max Müller-Eberstein","Masaru Isonuma"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["training data influence","pretraining data analysis","data curriculum","gradient-based attribution","pythia"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13515","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.13515","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.13545","title":"LittleLearner: Language Models Under Pedagogically Controlled Knowledge Exposure","abstract":"Modern language models are trained on heterogeneous web-scale text corpora. Consequently, studying knowledge and skill acquisition is difficult, as prior exposure to related content is hard to characterize. To address this challenge, we introduce LITTLECURRICULUM, a curated 88B-token pretraining corpus tailored to U.S. elementary school material, explicitly excluding concepts, facts, and vocabulary taught above Grade 5. Training a 5B-parameter LLM from scratch on LITTLECURRICULUM yields LITTLELE","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T17:56:12Z","category_id":"pretraining_data","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13545.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要解决的不是单纯“再做一个小模型”，而是给语言模型研究提供一个可控的预训练暴露环境。现有 web-scale 语料里，模型到底见过什么知识、词汇、概念和推理形式，通常说不清，因此很难判断 ICL、SFT 或其他后训练方法到底是在激活已有能力，还是让模型真正学到了新东西。LittleLearner 试图把这个问题前移到预训练阶段：直接构造一个严格限制在美国 K–5 小学课程范围内的语料，让模型的知识边界、语言复杂度边界和能力边界都尽量可解释、可验证。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要有三层。第一，它把“数据边界可控”这件事做成了一个可复现实验平台，而不是停留在 benchmark 去污染或 OOD 评测层面。第二，它提出了一种面向能力归因的语料构建思路：不是追求覆盖率，而是追求高精度地排除超出目标边界的内容，这对研究知识获取、能力涌现、数据补丁、continual pretrain 都有直接价值。第三，它提醒人注意一个常被忽略的问题：如果想研究模型如何获得新知识，训练集的负空间同样重要。LittleCurriculum 的价值不在于“更强”，而在于“更干净地知道模型没见过什么”。从贡献量级看，它更像一个研究基础设施和方法学资产，而不是追求性能的 recipe 论文。","technical_approach":"方法核心是一个以高精度过滤为目标的多阶段数据管线，用 FineWeb-Edu 为底座，把语料切成 K–5 与 Beyond-K–5 两部分。第一层是基于 Age-of-Acquisition（AoA）的轻量规则过滤。作者发现 AoA 比其他语言难度代理更能区分年级，但 FineWeb-Edu 中约 10% 词没有 AoA 标注，于是用 Zipf 词频上的线性模型补全缺失值。过滤时不是因为单个高难词就丢弃文档，而是要求超过 5% 的词年龄超过 12 岁才剔除，这样能减少单词级异常值带来的误杀。第二层是 LLM-as-a-judge 标注少量样本，再训练分类器扩展到全量语料。这里先用 FastText 做便宜的粗筛，再对约 2.66 亿个与 AoA 年级带一致的样本用成本高约 50 倍的 ModernBERT 做强分类。这个设计很务实：如果直接全量 LLM 标注，按 Gemini Flash 价格估算要约 4600 万美元，因此必须把 LLMJ 只用在“造教师数据”，而不是直接做全量判别。第三层是规则式符号过滤，专门处理分类器不容易学到、但又明显超出小学边界的数学符号和公式，例如 ∑、∫、∂、二次表达式等。作者强调这一步只去掉后续保留文档中的约 0.1%，说明它不是大规模数学过滤器，而是清理 notation-heavy 的残余尾部。第四层是基于术语的保守收紧。因为过滤后语料仍大于训练目标规模，作者没有均匀下采样，而是利用前面阶段得到的年级标签，找出与 Beyond-K–5 显著相关的术语，再删除包含这些术语的候选文档。这里的逻辑很关键：既然稀疏出现也可能让模型学到事实，那在研究受控暴露时，宁可多删也不要留下边界泄漏。最后，作者用 CommonCoreText 这个带真实年级标签的 held-out 数据集做验证。整体设计取舍很明确：precision-first，而不是 recall-first。也就是说，他们接受大量真正 K–5 文本被错杀，只要能更有把握地保证保留下来的语料不越界。","experiments":"最关键的结果不是下游分数，而是过滤边界的可验证性。作者在 CommonCoreText 上报告，管线能把 Beyond-K–5 内容的保留率压到 0%，同时保留约 35% 的 K–5 内容。这个 35% 看起来不高，但正是他们刻意选择高精度过滤的结果：在真实 K–5 文本上都有 65% 拒绝率，说明对噪声更大的 FineWeb-Edu 只会更保守。语料规模方面，最终 LittleCurriculum 为 88B tokens，并据此从零训练一个 5B 参数模型 LittleLearner。摘要里提到该模型已经具备开放式评测所需的基本语言能力，但同时保留了清晰的知识与能力边界。全文的重点不是把 LittleLearner 做到多强，而是展示边界控制后，模型在超出 K–5 的知识、词汇和推理要求上会出现可解释的缺口，从而为后续研究 ICL、post-training、数据补丁和能力扩展提供干净起点。实验上更值得注意的是过滤流程本身的成本与分工：LLMJ 只用于少量标注，FastText 做大规模廉价筛选，ModernBERT 做高质量复判，规则过滤补尾部漏洞，这种 cascade 很适合大规模预训练数据清洗场景。","reading_guide":"泛读时先抓三部分。第一看 Introduction，明确作者要解决的是“能力归因”而不是“小学模型”这个表面问题。第二看数据构建部分，尤其是 AoA 过滤、LLMJ 到分类器蒸馏、以及最后的术语收紧，这三步决定了边界是否可信。第三看验证部分和 Figure 2，理解为什么作者宁可牺牲大量召回，也要把 Beyond-K–5 retention 压到 0%。如果时间有限，优先读主文的数据管线和附录里 CommonCoreText、prompt 优化、symbolic filtering 的细节，预计 30–45 分钟能把方法和取舍读明白。值得深挖的点有两个：一是这种 precision-first 语料是否会引入文体单一、分布过窄的问题；二是 AoA、词频、术语黑名单这些代理变量，和真正“概念边界”之间还有多大偏差。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper is not merely about training a smaller model on simpler text. Its real target is controllable pretraining exposure. In standard web-scale corpora, it is hard to know what a model has already seen, which makes it difficult to tell whether in-context learning, SFT, or other post-training methods genuinely add new capability or only elicit latent knowledge. LittleLearner moves the control point to pretraining itself: build a corpus restricted to U.S. K–5 material so that the model’s knowledge boundary, vocabulary boundary, and reasoning boundary become much more interpretable.","importance":"Its value to pretraining research is methodological rather than leaderboard-oriented. First, it provides a reproducible sandbox for studying capability acquisition under explicit exposure constraints, instead of relying only on contamination-aware benchmarks or OOD evaluation. Second, it frames dataset construction around attribution: not maximizing coverage, but maximizing confidence about what the model did not see. That is directly useful for studying knowledge acquisition, data patches, continual pretraining, and the interface between pretrain and post-train. Third, it highlights an underappreciated point: if the goal is to study how models gain new knowledge, the negative space of the corpus matters as much as the positive space. The contribution is best viewed as research infrastructure plus a data methodology, not as a stronger training recipe.","technical_approach":"$17","experiments":"$18","reading_guide":"For a skim, focus on three parts. criteria","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T13:03:49Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13200.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI","cs.IR"],"summary":{"core_problem":"GEM 解决的是 dense retriever 对复杂自然语言需求理解不足的问题。用户现在会用带推理、约束和偏好的方式表达检索需求，但传统 embedding 模型主要编码表层语义相似度，难以显式理解用户意图和相关性标准。","importance":"这篇工作对预训练研究的启发在于，它把生成式语言建模和表示学习放进同一个训练目标，而不是把 LLM reasoning 和 retriever 做成两个模型的流水线。它关心的问题接近“同一个 decoder-only backbone 能否同时保留生成能力和产出高质量 embedding”。这对未来统一模型接口有参考意义：模型可以先生成可解释的中间推理，再把这个推理压入向量空间，用于检索、记忆访问或工具路由。它不是通用 LLM 预训练配方，但在生成-表示联合训练上有方法价值。","technical_approach":"GEM 采用 generate-then-encode 范式。给定查询 q，系统先用一个 meta-instruction 组成 prompt p，引导模型生成关于用户意图和相关性标准的 reasoning response r。随后模型对 p 与 r 的拼接内容进行编码，并通过附加 embedding token 输出检索向量。训练上，它同时使用 contrastive loss 和 causal language modeling loss：前者让 embedding 能区分正负文档，后者避免模型在变成 bi-encoder 后遗忘生成能力。数据构造是关键。对每个查询，先采样多个候选 reasoning，再用 LLM relevance classifier 检查原始正例文档在该 reasoning 下是否仍然相关，过滤掉误解查询的推理。之后构造与 reasoning 一致的正例，以及主题相近但在细节上与 reasoning 冲突的 hard negative，迫使模型不能只靠关键词或主题相似度完成检索。","experiments":"论文在 reasoning-intensive retrieval 和 instruction-following retrieval 任务上评估 GEM。已给出的内容显示，GEM 的自生成 reasoning 能提升检索效果，并且通过测试时 prompting 增加推理计算还能继续带来增益。全文片段没有提供具体数据集名称、模型规模、batch size、训练数据量、NDCG/Recall 数字或消融表，因此只能确认实验方向，不能确认提升幅度。最值得看的消融应包括：无 reasoning 的普通 embedding、外部 LLM 生成 reasoning 的 pipeline、只用 contrastive loss、不加 causal LM loss、不使用 reasoning-aligned hard negatives，以及不同采样数量 K 对数据质量的影响。","reading_guide":"建议泛读偏精读，预计 30–40 分钟。重点看 Generative Embedding Model 的整体结构、embedding token 的使用方式、联合损失函数，以及 Figure 2 的 reasoning 数据生成流程。实验部分优先看 reasoning-intensive retrieval 与 instruction-following retrieval 的主表，再看 joint training 和 hard negative 构造的消融。对预训练研究员而言，最值得深挖的是 contrastive objective 与 causal LM objective 是否真的兼容，以及这种 generate-then-encode 接口能否扩展到长上下文记忆、RAG query rewriting 或 tool routing。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"GEM addresses a mismatch between modern user queries and conventional dense retrieval. Users increasingly express information needs through instructions, constraints, and implicit reasoning, while most embedding models still rely on lexical or shallow semantic similarity.","importance":"The work is relevant because it trains a single model to both generate reasoning and produce retrieval embeddings. Instead of using an external LLM to rewrite or explain the query before passing it to a separate retriever, GEM asks the retriever itself to reason and then encode that reasoning. This is useful for foundation-model research on unified generation-and-representation training. It is not a general LLM pretraining recipe, but it gives a concrete design for preserving causal generation while learning a contrastive embedding space.","technical_approach":"GEM uses a generate-then-encode interface. Given a query, it prepends a meta-instruction that asks the model to reason about user intent and relevance criteria. The model generates a response, then encodes the concatenation of prompt and response, with an appended embedding token used as the retrieval representation. Training combines a contrastive retrieval objective with a causal language modeling objective, so the model learns document discrimination without losing its generative behavior. The data pipeline is central: for each query, multiple candidate reasoning traces are sampled; an LLM-based relevance classifier checks whether the original positive document remains relevant under each trace; invalid reasoning is filtered out. The remaining reasoning is used to build positives aligned with the inferred intent and hard negatives that share topics but subtly violate the reasoning, reducing dependence on surface matching.","experiments":"The paper evaluates GEM on reasoning-intensive retrieval and instruction-following retrieval. The supplied content indicates that self-generated reasoning improves retrieval and that test-time prompting can further improve performance by spending more compute on reasoning. The available text does not provide model sizes, training scale, dataset names, or metric values such as NDCG or recall, so the magnitude of gains cannot be assessed from the provided material. The most informative ablations would compare against plain embedding without reasoning, external-LLM reasoning pipelines, contrastive-only training, removal of the causal LM loss, removal of reasoning-aligned hard negatives, and different numbers of sampled reasoning candidates.","reading_guide":"Skim with some attention to the method, about 30–40 minutes. Read the model interface, the embedding-token design, the joint loss, and the reasoning-conditioned data synthesis pipeline. In the experiments, focus on the main retrieval tables for reasoning-intensive and instruction-following settings, then inspect ablations on joint training and hard negative construction. For pretraining researchers, the key question is whether causal LM loss and contrastive embedding loss remain compatible at scale, and whether the same generate-then-encode pattern can support memory retrieval, RAG query planning, or tool routing.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"值得看的是“先生成再嵌入”这个接口设计，不一定能直接迁到大规模 pretrain，但它提醒你检索表征未必该被 next-token 目标单独决定；先看方法图和主表即可。","en":"The interesting part is the generate-then-embed interface: it may not transfer cleanly to large-scale pretraining, but it challenges the prior that retrieval representations should be shaped only by standard LM objectives; read the method figure and main table."},"metadata":{"institutions":["University of Glasgow","Affiliation:University of Glasgow"],"authors":["Zhili Shen","Craig Macdonald"],"notable_authors":["Craig Macdonald"],"tags":["retrieval","generative-embedding","reasoning-aware-retrieval","test-time-compute","representation-learning"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13200","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.13200","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.13156","title":"Rethinking Normalization Placement for LLMs: Post-Norm under Curriculum Depth Growing","abstract":"Pre-norm is the standard normalization placement in modern Transformers because it facilitates joint optimization of full-depth models. We ask whether this preference persists when depth is introduced through a curriculum. In curriculum depth growth, each appended block receives the boundary representation produced by a trained prefix, making normalization placement relevant to forward conditioning. We therefore test whether placement and training curriculum interact. token，训练和部署都会遇到 memory-time tradeoff。TAM 的结论说明，推理轨迹中的 token 并不等价；问题定义、中间结论和关键计算锚点比探索性或废弃路径更应保留。这对后续研究有两个影响：一是可以把 reasoning trace 看成可压缩的分层状态，而不是必须完整保留的文本；二是训练阶段可能需要让模型生成更可分段、更可压缩的推理轨迹。","technical_approach":"TAM 建在 Attention Matching 之上。Attention Matching 的目标不是简单丢 token，而是构造更短的 compact KV，使其在参考 query 上同时匹配局部 attention output 和 attention mass。TAM 加了三个结构化机制。第一，thought segmentation，把已经生成的推理轨迹切成若干 reasoning blocks。第二，adaptive budget allocation，根据每个 block 的重要性权重和长度分配 compact token 数，理论上给出 t_i ∝ sqrt(w_i n_i) 的分配规则；这比统一压缩更适合误差随预算递减的场景。第三，pivotal token protection，保留高 attention 的关键 token，避免被压缩过程抹掉。参考 query 来自最近 selector window，使方法适合 mid-trajectory compaction，而不是只在 prefill 阶段处理静态上下文。底层拟合仍使用 AM 的闭式或轻量求解流程，包括 attention mass 的非负最小二乘和 value 的普通最小二乘。","experiments":"实验使用 Qwen3-4B，在 AIME 2024 和 MATH-500 上评估长推理场景下的压缩效果。作者报告 TAM 在相同 memory budget 下相比 uniform compaction 有更稳定的准确率收益；周期性 compaction 最高可降低约 65% peak memory。消融设计围绕三个部件展开：分段、按重要性分配预算、保护 pivotal tokens。核心结果是，单纯 uniform compression 在高压缩率下更容易损失关键 reasoning anchor，而 TAM 通过把预算集中在高权重 block 和关键 token 上，能在较低 KV 内存下保留更多解题性能。文中还给出理论支撑：预算分配规则在凸误差模型下最优，保护 pivotal token 可降低近似误差，连续多次压缩的累计误差有界。","reading_guide":"泛读建议用 20 到 30 分钟。先看 Introduction 中对 AM、RPC 和 TAM 三者差异的定位：compaction primitive、query source、structure-aware budget allocation。然后看 Preliminaries 里 attention mass matching 的分解，这是理解为什么 AM 不等于 token eviction 的关键。方法部分重点读 segmentation、importance score、t_i ∝ sqrt(w_i n_i) 分配规则，以及 pivotal token 的定义。实验部分主要看不同压缩率下的 accuracy-memory 曲线和 ablation。若只关心 serving，可重点看 peak-memory reduction 和 compaction 周期；若关心预训练或推理建模，可重点看推理轨迹的分层结构假设。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets KV-cache growth during long chain-of-thought decoding. Existing cache compaction methods usually treat the generated reasoning trace as a flat token sequence and apply a uniform compression policy. TAM argues that reasoning traces have block structure, and that different blocks should receive different cache budgets because their relevance to future generation varies substantially.","importance":"The work is mainly about inference efficiency, but it has useful implications for reasoning-model training. Long reasoning is not only a capability problem; it also creates a memory and bandwidth problem. TAM shows that generated reasoning tokens are not equally useful: problem statements, intermediate results, and conclusion anchors deserve more preservation than abandoned explorations. This supports a view of reasoning traces as compressible hierarchical state, which may influence how models are trained to produce structured, reusable, and cache-friendly reasoning trajectories.","technical_approach":"TAM extends Attention Matching rather than relying on simple token eviction. Attention Matching constructs a shorter set of compact keys and values that match both local attention outputs and attention mass under a set of reference queries. TAM adds three mechanisms. First, it segments the generated trajectory into thought blocks. Second, it allocates the compression budget adaptively using each block’s importance and length, with an allocation rule of the form t_i proportional to sqrt(w_i n_i). Third, it protects pivotal tokens, identified as high-attention anchors, so that important reasoning states are not removed by the compaction step. Reference queries are drawn from a recent selector window, making the method suitable for mid-trajectory compaction during decoding. The underlying fitting still follows the AM pipeline, including nonnegative least squares for attention-mass matching and ordinary least squares for value fitting.","experiments":"The evaluation uses Qwen3-4B on AIME 2024 and MATH-500. TAM improves accuracy over uniform compaction at the same memory budget, and periodic compaction reduces peak memory by up to about 65%. The ablations are organized around the three main components: thought segmentation, adaptive budget allocation, and pivotal-token protection. The reported pattern is consistent with the method’s premise: uniform compression loses important reasoning anchors at high compression ratios, while TAM spends more budget on high-importance blocks and preserves key tokens. The paper also provides theoretical support: the allocation rule is optimal under a convex error model, pivotal-token protection reduces approximation error, and sequential compaction has bounded cumulative error.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes about 20 to 30 minutes. s","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T08:32:18Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_12953.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"结构化剪枝在 LLM 上常见两类问题：只做 depth（删层/删块）很难精确命中目标压缩比；只做 width（删头/删通道）容易造成不规则张量形状、实际加速有限。更关键的是，多数方法用贪心局部决策，忽略跨层依赖，导致性能波动大、对校准集分布敏感。论文要解决的是：能否把 depth 与 width 统一成一个可控预算的全局选择问题，并在给定重要性估计的前提下给出“有保证”的最优选择，同时把压缩比命中率做成可量化指标。","importance":"这篇属于推理效率/模型压缩方向，对预训练本身的直接贡献不大，但对“如何在不重训的情况下把大模型裁剪到可部署形态”有工程价值。对基座研究的启发主要在两点：\n- 与其子项。\n2) Depth 阶段 = 0/1 背包：每个可选组件 x 有 weight w(x)（参数量）与 value v(x)（重要性/贡献）。在目标参数预算 C 下最大化总 value。用动态规划求解，并通过参数量离散化因子 α 把复杂度从 O(N·C) 降到 O(N·⌊C/α⌋)。作者强调这是“相对于固定重要性估计的条件最优”，比贪心至少多一个可解释的最优性基线。\n3) Dispatchers balance token counts (EPLB, LPLB, UltraEP) or activated-expert counts (METRO), assuming expert time is linear in one. Measurements on two datacenter GPU generations show it is neither: below $\\nstar\\!\\approx\\!156$--$168$ tokens, HBM weight streaming dominates---cost attaches to \\emph{activated replicas}, not tokens; above it, grouped GEMM rounds tokens to 128-tile $M$-tiles, so \\emph{splitting} an exper","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T10:21:11Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13057.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.DC","cs.AI","cs.CL","cs.GT"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是 MoE 推理里 expert-parallel dispatch 的目标函数错配问题。现有做法通常用 token 数或 activated expert 数做代理，默认单个 GPU 的 expert 计算时间分别近似线性依赖于 token 数或 expert 数。但作者直接测量后发现，真实时延由两类硬件效应共同决定：一是小 token 区间里权重从 HBM 流入带来的固定开销，二是 grouped GEMM 的 tile padding 让 token 成本呈台阶状而不是线性。由于 EP 每层在 all-to-all 前后都要同步，真正决定吞吐的是最慢 GPU 的 makespan，而不是平均负载。问题的难点不在单一 regime，而在真实 decode batch 中 memory-bound 的冷 expert 与 compute-bound 的热 expert 同时存在，导致任何单一 proxy 都会系统性失真。","importance":"对大模型基座研究的意义主要在两点。第一，它把 MoE serving 中常被工程经验处理的 dispatch 问题，明确改写成一个由 kernel 级测量驱动的 makespan 优化问题，说明 proxy-based balancing 为什么会在现代 GPU 上失效。第二，它给出一个很清楚的信号：MoE 的系统优化不能只看 token-level load，也不能只看 activation count，训练侧若继续追求更强路由稀疏性、更大 expert、更高复制率，推理侧的最优 dispatch 目标会越来越依赖硬件 cost model。这对 pretrain 研究也有启发，因为 MoE 架构设计、capacity factor、expert size、replication 策略，都会通过 serving kernel 的 regime 切换反过来影响可部署性。","technical_approach":"方法分三层。第一层是 cost model。作者对部署 kernel 做黑盒标定，用很短时间测出每个 expert 在不同 token 数下的执行时间，并用一个两段式模型拟合：小 token 区间由每次激活 replica 的固定成本主导，大 token 区间再叠加近似线性的 compute 成本；同时再显式加入 grouped GEMM 的 M-tile padding，解释为什么把一个 expert 的 token 切碎到多个 replica 上会凭空制造额外计算。这个模型里，b 对应每多实例化一个 replica 的权重流式加载成本，另一个参数刻画 padding 台阶。第二层是问题形式化。给定 batch 内各 expert 的路由 token、各 replica 的 placement，目标是最小化所有 GPU 上 modeled time 的最大值。作者证明这个 fixed-charge makespan dispatch 问题在很小规模下就已 NP-hard，但在 b→0 或 padding 消失等退化情形会退回已有 token LP 或 activation balancing，这个分析把旧方法放到了统一框架里。第三层是算法 tempo_fast。它不是求全局最优，而是围绕 cost model 做多阶段近似：先做 cost-aware seeding 初始化，再用 augmenting-chain 做 activation rebalancing，优先处理 flat regime 下多开 replica 的固定成本；然后在 bottleneck GPU 周围做 local search 和 partial migration，处理 linear regime 下热 expert 的 token 搬移；最后把 token-LP 解和 round-robin 证书一起放到同一 cost model 下打分，做一个小型 ensemble 选最优。这个设计的关键不是某个单独技巧，而是每一步都对应一个明确的硬件机制，而不是继续优化代理指标。","experiments":"实验最有价值的是测量和误差分析。作者在两代数据中心 GPU 上测得单 expert FFN 时间在大约 n*=156–168 tokens 之前基本平坦，之后才进入近似线性区间。拟合得到的 activation floor b 从 1.74 微秒到 14.8 微秒，随 expert shape 变化可达 8.5 倍，说明 expert 大小会显著改变 dispatch 的最优策略。模型拟合误差均值在 4–8% 左右，说明这个黑盒 cost model 足够用于在线决策。真实 trace 上，92–100% 的 decode batch 同时包含两种 regime：少数热 expert 承载约 91% token，很多冷 expert 却仍停留在 flat regime。只改 split、不改 batch 和 placement 时，现有 proxy policy 产生的 modeled block time 可相差 1.2–1.6 倍，p95 达 1.7 倍；每种 proxy 在自己不擅长的 regime 会多付 30–70% 成本。算法层面，tempo_fast 相对最优或强基线的 gap 被压到约 1.02% Existing dispatch policies balance token counts or activated-expert counts, implicitly assuming expert time is linear in one of them. The full paper shows this assumption breaks on real GPUs for two hardware reasons: a per-replica memory-bound weight-streaming cost dominates at low token counts, and grouped GEMM introduces tile-padding overhead so token cost is staircase-like rather than linear. Since each MoE layer synchronizes at all-to-all boundaries, the relevant objective is the per-batch makespan of the slowest GPU, not average load. The hard case is not one regime or the other, but mixed batches where hot experts are compute-bound while many cold experts remain memory-bound.","importance":"The contribution matters because it reframes MoE serving dispatch from proxy balancing into measured cost-model optimization. We propose Reduced Matrix Multiplication (RMM), a training-free, input-adaptive inference method that reduces Transformer matrix products by selecting informative slices along their contraction dimensions, without modifying model weights. Under a simple retention-ratio control, RMM provides a smooth and predictable accuracy-efficiency trade-of","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T16:16:04Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13426.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作问的是一个更细粒度的推理冗余问题：Transformer 推理里，矩阵乘法共享收缩维上的所有分量是否都必须为每个输入完整计算。现有高效推理方法多从结构剪枝、低秩、token 压缩、KV 管理或激活稀疏化入手，但较少直接在每次矩阵乘法的 contraction dimension 上做输入自适应裁剪。作者试图给出一个训练后、无需改权重的统一近似框架，用来回答不同模型、模块和任务到底能容忍多少这类乘法约减。","importance":"对基座研究的价值更多在分析和启发，而不是立即可用的通用加速方案。它把 attention 和 MLP 中看似不同的计算统一写成 AB 形式，然后研究 shared dimension 的可约减性，得到两个对模型设计有用的观察：一是 I，只计算 A 的对应列与 B 的对应行，从而把完整乘法替换成 reduced product。与经典 approximate matrix multiplication 不同，作者不做随机采样，因为单次推理无法靠多次采样平均误差；而是采用确定性的、输入自适应的 Top-K 选择。具体地，用当前输入激活矩阵 A 在每个共享维度 j 上的列范数 ||A_:,j||_2 作为重要性分数，保留分数最高的 rho*d 个维度。这样做的直觉是：若某个维度在当前输入上的激活能量很低，它对输出贡献通常也较小，可以暂时跳过。这个框架可套到多种 Transformer 乘法上，包括线性投影、MLP、大致也包括 attention score 的 head feature 维和 attention-value 乘法中的 token 维。论文强调它不改模型参数、不需要再训练，只通过 retention ratio rho 控制精度-效率折中。工程上作者还实现了 Triton kernel，验证 reduced product 不只是理论 FLOPs 下降，而能转成一定 wall-clock 收益。","experiments":"实验覆盖 1B 到 70B 的语言模型，以及部分多模态 VLM 推理场景。全文给出的主要结论不是某个单点数字，而是几条稳定趋势。第一，模型家族、任务类型、被约减的模块和 retention ratio 都会显著影响容忍度，没有单一全局最优配置。第二，在中等 reduction 下，大模型通常比小模型更稳，说明规模提升带来一定冗余缓冲。第三，attention 相关乘法明显比 MLP 更可约减，MLP 对精度更敏感，这一点在不同任务上都较一致。第四，方法在判别式、生成式和多模态设置下都能工作，但收益和退化曲线差异较大，因此更像一个分析工具和可控近似算子，而不是即插即用的统一加速器。作者还通过 Triton 实现展示 reduced matrix product 可以带来实际时延下降，不过真实收益会受 Top-K 选择开销、kernel reduction 与实际加速之间的差距。若只想获取研究启发，20–30 分钟足够；若想评估能否用于生产推理，需要额外关注 Top-K 本身的代价、prefill 与 decode 是否分开处理、以及不同矩阵形状下 kernel 是否仍高效。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks whether every index along the contraction dimension of Transformer matrix products must be evaluated at inference time for every input. Prior efficiency work usually targets pruning, low-rank structure, token compression, KV-cache management, or activation sparsity. RMM instead studies input-adaptive reduction directly inside each matrix multiplication, without changing model weights.","importance":"Its value is more diagnostic than definitive. The paper provides a unified lens for measuring reducibility across attention and MLP computations, and the main takeaway is structurally useful: attention-side products are often much more reducible than MLP products, and larger models tend to tolerate moderate reduction better than smaller ones. For foundation-model researchers, that suggests where inference redundancy may actually live and where training-aware sparsification or architecture changes might be most promising.","technical_approach":"RMM rewrites Transformer computations in the generic form Y = AB and reduces the shared dimension d by selecting a subset of indices rather than evaluating the full product. The selection is deterministic and input-adaptive. For each shared-dimension index j, the method computes an importance score from the current activation matrix, specifically the column norm ||A_:,j||_2, and keeps the top rho fraction of indices. The reduced product is then formed using only the selected columns of A and rows of B. This differs from classical approximate matrix multiplication, which relies on randomized sampling and expectation over repeated draws; that is a poor fit for single-pass inference. The method is training-free, weight-preserving, and controlled by a simple retention ratio. The same idea is applied to multiple Transformer products, including attention-side and MLP-side multiplications. The authors also implement Triton kernels to test whether FLOP reduction can translate into wall-clock gains.","experiments":"The evaluation spans language models from 1B to 70B parameters and also includes multimodal VLM inference. The main findings are trend-level rather than centered on one headline number. Reduction tolerance depends strongly on model family, task, component, and retention ratio. Larger models are often more robust under moderate reduction. Attention-related products are consistently more reducible, while MLP computations are much more sensitive. The method works across discriminative, autoregressive, and multimodal settings, but the quality-efficiency curve varies substantially, so RMM looks more like a controllable approximation tool than a universal acceleration recipe. The Triton implementation shows that reduced products can yield practical speedups, though realized gains depend on selection overhead, kernel quality, and hardware specifics.","reading_guide":"For a skim read, first inspect the method section where all Transformer computations are cast into the same AB template. Conventional width or depth scaling raises costs for both phases proportionally. Existing test-time compute scaling methods (e.g. chain-of-thought, pause tokens) either add sequence length overhead or require modified persistent state, and cannot independently scale decode-side computation while preserving the standard prefill path and KV cache structure.","importance":"This work introduces a new architectural path for test-time compute scaling, with concrete implications for pretraining research: first, the shared-weight primary/auxiliary flow design provides a reference for deployment-aware architecture design, as it improves prediction quality via extra decode compute without changing the base model's prefill path or KV cache interface. Second, independent expert fan-out configuration for primary and auxiliary flows in MoE models allows a single pretrained checkpoint to adapt to deployment workloads with different prefill/decode ratios. Third, the mixture likelihood training objective is a reusable design for supervising multi-path models.","technical_approach":"$1d","experiments":"Experiments cover three settings: controlled NanoGPT data scaling, dense LLaMA-style model size scaling, and sparse MoE base configurations. PagedAttention uses fixed-size memory blocks to reduce allocator-level fragmentation, but recent KV eviction algorithms operate at a token granularity finer than block-level management. This mismatch causes intra-block fragmentation, leaving a large fraction of allocated KV memory unreclaimable. We present vToken, a lightweight token-level virtualization layer that decouples l","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-08-13T14:01:21Z","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2608_13263.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI","cs.DC","cs.OS"],"summary":{"core_problem":"PagedAttention 以固定大小块管理 KV 缓存，而 H2O、StreamingLLM、Scissorhands 等 KV 驱逐算法工作在 token 粒度，两者粒度不匹配导致块内碎片化：当一个块同时包含被驱逐和被保留的 token 时，该块无法释放，大量已分配但利用率低的内存无法被其他请求复用。","importance":"在 token 级驱逐策略与块级 KV 管理之间补上了缺失的运行时抽象层，既保留了 PagedAttention 的内核效率与 CUDA Graph 兼容性，又让任意 token 级驱逐策略能真正转化为可回收的物理内存，对长上下文高并发 serving 系统的容量与工程迭代效率都有直接提升。","technical_approach":"在驱逐策略与 PagedAttention 之间插入 vToken 轻量级 token 级虚拟化层。向上暴露稳定的 per-sequence 逻辑 token 视图，驱逐策略只需指定保留哪些 token，无需感知物理块布局；向下维护逻辑 token 到物理槽位的映射表（token-table indirection），当块内碎片化达到可操作阈值时，异步将存活 token 重打包（repack / lazy compaction）到更少的物理块中，释放空闲块。整个设计不修改 attention kernel，兼容现有 PagedAttention 实现与 CUDA Graph。","experiments":"在 vLLM 中实现 vToken，跨多个模型与 H2O、StreamingLLM、Scissorhands 三种驱逐策略评测。核心结果：相比朴素将驱逐策略直接接到 vLLM 的 Naive-Evict，vToken 减少 16%–85% 的保留 KV 块数；在 SLA 约束下吞吐最高提升 1.37×；在活跃 KV 预算受限场景下，最大可行并发数最高扩展 2×；工程上，新驱逐策略的集成代码量从 500+ 行降到 50 行以内。","reading_guide":"泛读约 20 分钟。重点看 §2.3 对粒度不匹配导致块内碎片化的量化分析，§3 中 token-table indirection 与异步 repack 的具体设计，以及 §4 中不同驱逐策略下的内存回收与吞吐收益。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"PagedAttention manages KV caches in fixed-size blocks, while KV eviction algorithms such as H2O, StreamingLLM, and Scissorhands operate at token granularity. This granularity mismatch causes intra-block fragmentation: when a block contains both evicted and retained tokens, the block cannot be freed, leaving a large fraction of allocated but underutilized memory unreclaimable by other requests.","importance":"vToken fills the missing runtime abstraction layer between token-level eviction policies and block-level KV management. It preserves the kernel efficiency and CUDA Graph compatibility of PagedAttention, while enabling arbitrary token-level eviction policies to actually translate into reclaimable physical memory. It directly improves both capacity and engineering iteration velocity for long-context, high-concurrency LLM serving systems.","technical_approach":"vToken inserts a lightweight token-level virtualization layer between eviction policies and the PagedAttention substrate. Upward, it exposes a stable per-sequence logical token view so eviction policies only specify which tokens to retain, without reasoning about physical block layout. Downward, it maintains a logical-to-physical mapping via token-table indirection; when intra-block fragmentation reaches an actionable threshold, it asynchronously repacks (lazy compacts) live tokens into fewer physical blocks and releases the freed ones. The design requires no attention-kernel modifications and is compatible with existing PagedAttention implementations and CUDA Graph.","experiments":"vToken is implemented in vLLM and evaluated across multiple models and three eviction policies: H2O, StreamingLLM, and Scissorhands. Key results: compared with Naive-Evict (directly attaching eviction policies to vanilla vLLM), vToken reduces retained KV blocks by 16%–85%; it improves SLA-constrained throughput by up to 1.37×; under a constrained active-KV budget, it extends maximum feasible concurrency by up to 2×; on the engineering side, integration code for a new eviction policy drops from 500+ lines to under 50 lines.","reading_guide":"Skim in roughly 20 minutes. Causal correction heads restore this along a single chain, while draft trees broaden candidate coverage. The training-free reuse of a pretrained correction head has practical deployment value.","technical_approach":"DARTree extends a pretrained causal correction head from a single chain to a tree. The key change replaces node-by-node best-first heap expansion with depth-wise batched expansion: at each depth a fixed number of nodes are expanded and their corrected child distributions are computed in one batch, eliminating the interleaving of correction-head inference with heap operations. Because widening a layer adds little latency, the method first builds a wider supertree and then applies a deferred, simplified best-first pruning to obtain a compact verification tree under a fixed node budget. Correction still propagates along each branch path, preserving path-dependent scoring. The entire method is training-free and reuses an existing drafter and correction head.","experiments":"Evaluated on Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-8B across seven math, code, and chat benchmarks at temperatures 0 and 1 (four model–temperature configurations). Consistency：同一问题重复解码10次、系统提示追加无意义随机字符串、正反论点顺序互换，这些都不提供新信息，测试的是机械层面的一致性底线；（2）Single-turn Conviction：在judge给出判决后进行一次实质性质疑，测试单次挑战下的信念强度；（3）Multi-turn rate，还对每次翻案标注为corrective（靠近金标）或corrupting（远离金标），把稳定性和准确率方向区分开。实验覆盖9个frontier模型、14个评审任务，数据集涉及安全分类、毒性检测、红队评测、AI生成文本检测和政治响应评估，同时跑Binary和Likert两种打分方式。","experiments":"核心实验结果有几条。第一，所有被测模型在所有数据集和评分制式下都有可观的wiggle rate，judge不稳定是普遍现象，不是个别模型或个别任务的问题。第二，Binary与Likert在相同样本上表现出相反的翻转方向倾向，说明评分制式本身会系统性改变judge在压力下的行为，不是中立的测量工具。第三，发生wiggle时，翻案多数是corrupting而不是corrective，即压力更可能把判决推离金标，而不是帮助模型纠错。第四，跨压力层级存在一定易感性相关，但不同数据集、不同模型之间的wiggle profile可迁移性有限，意味着不能用单一任务上的稳定率外推到其他judge场景。第五，在低成本可观测信号中，模型内部jury（同一模型多次采样或多视角判断）的基线一致性是item级别不稳定的较强预测因子，比自报置信度这类信号更实用。","reading_guide":"建议精读。先看Figure 1和框架定义部分，把三个维度和wiggle的操作化定义吃透，尤其是Binary/Likert阈值设计为什么要剔除小漂移；然后重点看第6节讨论，那里集中了真正对使用judge做训练和评测有影响的结论：翻案方向、评分制式偏差、wiggle profile的跨任务迁移性，以及为什么自洽/jury一致性是实用的不稳定探针。做reward modeling、偏好数据合成或benchmark建设的人，应特别关注‘corrupting flip居多’这一发现，它直接关系到用模型自我批评/迭代优化时的信号质量。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"LLM judges are routinely validated by average accuracy on golden datasets, but accuracy does not tell us whether a verdict remains stable under re-prompting, semantically invariant prompt changes, single challenges, or sustained multi-turn pressure, nor whether verdict flips move toward or away from ground truth.","importance":"This work addresses a core fragility in evaluation infrastructure and training-signal quality. If judges wobble even when no new information is provided, then using them for benchmark scoring, reward modeling, preference annotation, or agentic self-regulation injects systematic noise into the pretraining–posttraining loop. The contribution is not just another robustness checklist: Wiggle operationalizes judge confidence as decomposable behavioral measurements rather than relying on self-reported scores or token probabilities, and it shows that flips under pressure are predominantly corrupting rather than corrective. That finding is directly relevant to anyone using LLM judges to generate training signals or evaluate frontier systems.","technical_approach":"$1f","experiments":"All tested models exhibit substantial wiggle rates across datasets and grading schemes, indicating that judge instability is a widespread phenomenon rather than an edge case. Binary and Likert formats produce opposite directional flip tendencies on the same items, showing that the grading scale itself is not a neutral measurement instrument. When flips occur, they are more often corrupting than corrective: pressure is more likely to push the verdict away from the ground-truth label than to repair an initial mistake. Susceptibility to pressure is correlated across perturbation levels to some extent, but wiggle-rate profiles transfer poorly across datasets and models, so stability on one task cannot be safely extrapolated to others. Among low-cost signals, baseline agreement within a model jury is the strongest practical predictor of item-level epistemic instability, outperforming heuristics such as self-reported confidence.","reading_guide":"Read closely. Start with Figure 1 and the framework definition to internalize the three dimensions and the operational definition of a wiggle, especially why the Likert threshold is designed to ignore small drift. Then focus on Section 6, which contains the findings that matter most for using judges in training and evaluation: the corrupting nature of most flips, scale-induced bias, limited cross-task transfer of stability profiles, and the practical value of within-model agreement as an instability probe. Researchers working on reward modeling, synthetic preference data, or benchmark construction should pay particular attention to the corruptive-flip result, as it directly affects the reliability of self-critique and iterative model-improvement loops.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"极其重要的 LLM-as-a-Judge 脆弱性分析，证明了高准确率的 Judge 在对抗下极易翻盘（62-91%），直接影响 Reward Model 的可靠性，必须关注其测试框架。","en":"Crucial vulnerability analysis of LLM-as-a-Judge, proving that highly accurate judges easily flip under adversarial pressure (62-91%), directly impacting Reward Model reliability; the testing framework is a must-read."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Meta"],"authors":["Justin Zhao","Himaghna Bhattacharjee","Hannah Korevaar","Bhaktipriya Radharapu","Khalid El-Arini"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["LLM-as-a-Judge","Epistemic Stability","Robustness Evaluation","Adversarial Testing"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12645","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2608.12645","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2608.13136","title":"LigBench: A Unified and Human-Aligned Benchmark for LLM-based Research Idea Generation","abstract":"With the rapid advancement of large language models (LLMs), research idea generation has attracted increasing attention. Its relevance to foundation-model research is indirect but concrete: scientific ideation systems need evaluation protocols that separate novelty, soundness, contribution, and overall quality, instead of treating an idea as a single free-form text. The PAIR-IQ dataset may also be useful for training idea judges, reward models, or research-agent evaluators, though the primary contribution remains benchmarking.","technical_approach":"LigBench first converts each candidate idea into a formalized representation to reduce format and verbosity bias. It evaluates ideas along four dimensions: rating, contribution, soundness, and novelty, each on a 0–5 scale. For a target idea, the system retrieves similar papers or ideas from PAIR-IQ, asks an LLM to perform pairwise comparisons, and aggregates the judgments with existing debiased PAIR-IQ scores through an Elo-style score update process until convergence. The choice of pairwise comparison is sensible: relative preference judgments are usually less noisy than absolute scores and better match how reviewers compare research directions. PAIR-IQ contains more than 11,000 conference papers with structured idea representations and debiased evaluation scores.","experiments":"The provided content states that the benchmark was used to evaluate several strong LLMs and can expose fine-grained differences in generated idea quality across dimensions such as novelty, soundness, and contribution.
